<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that full ordinary descendant Virasoro constraints pass from a smooth projective complex base to the projectivization of any algebraic vector bundle of rank at least two. The bundle need not split and satisfies no positivity requirement. Assuming the full constraints on the base, the conclusion includes every genus, each individual integral curve class, and all cohomology insertions, including primitive and odd classes. The result also applies successively to towers of projective bundles.

- **claim-main-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $B$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$, and let $E$ be an algebraic vector bundle of rank $r\geq2$ on $B$. Let $X=\mathbb P_B(E)$ parametrize one-dimensional subspaces of the fibers of $E$. Then $$\mathsf V(B)\ \Longrightarrow\ \mathsf V(X).$$
  - Explanation: Full descendant Virasoro constraints on a smooth connected projective base imply the same constraints on the projectivization of any algebraic vector bundle of rank at least two. The bundle need not split and needs no positivity.
- **claim-projective-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-covariance)): Suppose $M$ and $M^{-1}$ admit quantized actions, and $A$ and $MAM^{-1}$ admit coefficientwise quadratic quantizations. If $\eta$ and $\eta'$ are the source and target pairings, then $$U_M\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)U_M^{-1} =\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_{\eta'}(MAM^{-1})+\text{a scalar}. \label{conv:covariance-equation}$$ Consequently $A$ is satisfied projectively by $Z$ if and only if $MAM^{-1}$ is satisfied projectively by $U_M Z$. Multiplying either potential by an invertible scalar does not change this condition.
  - Explanation: Conjugating a quantized quadratic operator by a quantized symplectic change of frame gives the quantization of the conjugated operator plus a scalar. Hence projective satisfaction is transported between frames.
- **claim-finite-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conv-finite-support)): Suppose a potential has finite descendant-index support at fixed curve coefficient, $\hbar$ power, and variable order, and has $\hbar$ powers bounded below at fixed curve coefficient and variable order. Let $A$ be an even infinitesimal symplectic mode operator, linear over the coefficient parameters and independent of $\hbar$. Assume its powers of $z$ have a finite lower bound and its order in $z\partial_z$ is finite at each filtration coefficient. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)$ acts coefficientwise on this potential. The same holds with additional formal filtration variables. Quantized actions of transformations equal to the identity modulo positive filtration, whose logarithms satisfy these mode bounds, are defined by their formal exponential series in this setting.
  - Explanation: Under finite descendant support and lower bounds on the powers of the genus parameter, quadratic mode operators with bounded loop powers act coefficientwise on the potential. Exponentials of transformations that are the identity modulo positive filtration are defined by their series.
- **claim-curve-labels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-curve-labels)): For $P=X$ or $W$, the map $$H_2(P;\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow H_2(B;\mathbb Z)\oplus\mathbb Z, \qquad d\longmapsto \left(\pi_*d,\int_d c_1(\mathcal O_P(1))\right) \label{geo:integral-splitting}$$ is an isomorphism, including torsion. An effective class has an effective base pushforward $\beta$ and a nonnegative tautological degree $l$.
  - Explanation: Integral curve classes of either projective bundle split exactly into a base class and a tautological degree, torsion included. Effective classes have effective base pushforward and nonnegative fiber degree.
- **claim-ancestor-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-factorization)): There are classes $u_F\in H_F$ with coefficients of positive Novikov filtration, for $F=B,X$, and a symplectic power series $R(z)\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(H^{\mathrm{fix}})[[z]]$, with $R-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ of positive Novikov filtration, such that, writing $$S_f(z)=S_{F,\mathrm{tw}}(u_F,z),\qquad S_{\mathrm{bl}}(z)=\bigoplus_{F=B,X}S_f(z),$$ one has $$\begin{aligned} S_W(z)&=R(z)S_{\mathrm{bl}}(z),\label{loc:S-factorization}\\ \mathcal A_W(0)&=c\,U_R \prod_{F=B,X}\mathcal A_{F,\mathrm{tw}}(u_F), \label{loc:A-factorization} \end{aligned}$$ where $c$ is an invertible scalar independent of ancestor variables. For every curve coefficient, $S_W(z)$ is the expansion at infinity of a rational function of $z$. In <a href="#loc:S-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:S-factorization">[loc:S-factorization]</a> that rational function is expanded at $z=0$; the equality is in Laurent series with a finite lower bound at each curve coefficient. Both $R$ and its inverse admit quantized actions in the coefficientwise completion used here.
  - Explanation: Localization on the auxiliary space factors its fundamental solution and ancestor potential through the twisted theories of the two fixed components, via an upper symplectic series that is the identity modulo positive filtration. The series and its inverse have quantized actions in the coefficientwise completion.
- **claim-leg-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-leg-geometry)): A moving leg has degree $m\geq1$ on a line joining a point $x\in X$ to its image $b=\pi(x)\in B$. Its map from $\mathbb P^1$ is totally ramified over $x$ and $b$, and its markings and nodes lie at these two points. For any specified marking and attachment status at the endpoints, the leg moduli is a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack, the $\mu_m$-gerbe $q_m:\mathfrak L_m\to X$ of $m$th roots of $\mathcal O_X(-1)$. Its endpoint evaluation maps are $q_m$ and $\pi\circ q_m:\mathfrak L_m\to B$.* *At an endpoint in $F$, the tangent line of the leg has equivariant first Chern class $$a=\frac{w_F\lambda}{m}+\nu,$$ where $\nu$ is an ordinary degree-two class on the leg stack. Every node involving a moving leg has nonzero smoothing character.
  - Explanation: A moving leg is a multiple cover of an orbit line joining a point of the projective bundle to its image in the base. Its moduli is a smooth proper root gerbe over the projective bundle, and all nodes involving it have nonzero smoothing character.
- **claim-vertex-conversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-vertex-conversion)): In the summed vertex correlators with background $\epsilon$, an insertion at a distinguished flag transforms as $$\label{loc:flag-dressing} \frac{h}{a-\psi}\quad\longmapsto\quad \frac{S(u,a)h}{a-\bar\psi}.$$ The transformation remains valid with additional powers of the ancestor class at that flag and with other distinguished flags; all evaluation classes at the flag are included in $h$ before applying $S(u,a)$. Moreover, up to a scalar independent of $t$, $$\label{loc:mixed-potential} \mathcal A(\epsilon;t)=\mathcal A(u;t+x).$$
  - Explanation: A descendant insertion at a flag becomes an ancestor insertion dressed by the fundamental solution. The mixed potential with descendant background equals an ancestor potential at a shifted point, up to a scalar.
- **claim-grading-calibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shift-grading-lemma)): With $S_W=S_W(0,z)$ in the convention fixed above, $$G_a=S_WG_dS_W^{-1}. \label{shift:grading-calibration}$$
  - Explanation: The ancestor-frame grading operator is the conjugate of the descendant-frame grading operator by the fundamental solution of the auxiliary space.
- **claim-shift-operator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shift-operator)): There is a shift operator $$T_a=A(z,\lambda,Q,y)e^{z\partial_\lambda}$$ on the small quantum-cohomology module of $W$, whose matrix $A$ is polynomial in $z,\lambda$ at every curve coefficient in the chosen equivariant basis. Its calibration is $$T_a=S_WT_dS_W^{-1}, \label{shift:calibration}$$ where, writing $v=x+w_F\lambda$ for the equivariant normal roots, $$T_d\big\|_F =y^{j_F} \prod_x \begin{cases} x+\lambda,& F=X,\\[2pt] (x-\lambda-z)^{-1},& F=B \end{cases} e^{z\partial_\lambda}, \qquad j_X=0,\quad j_B=1. \label{shift:fixed-formula}$$ All operators act on the full equivariant cohomology with its super pairing.
  - Explanation: A shift operator translating the equivariant parameter by the loop variable exists on the small quantum module of the auxiliary space, with polynomial matrix coefficients. It is calibrated by the fundamental solution against an explicit fixed-locus formula.
- **claim-shift-polynomiality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shift-polynomiality)): For every fixed actual base class $\beta$, the matrix coefficients of $A$ and of $c\star$ are polynomials in $y,z,\lambda$ (with no $z$ dependence in $c\star$). In particular they are holomorphic functions of $y$ before any localization in $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: At each fixed base class the matrix coefficients of the shift and of quantum multiplication by the first Chern class are polynomials in the fiber variable and parameters, hence holomorphic in the fiber variable.
- **claim-shift-commutation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shift-commutation-lemma)): The grading and shift commute: $$=0. \label{shift:commutation}$$
  - Explanation: The ancestor-frame grading commutes with the shift operator, checked in the descendant frame by cancelling contributions from the normal roots.
- **claim-fiber-shift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-shift-fiber-operator)): On $H_T^*(W)$ with the fiber quantum product, $$T_a\bmod(z,Q^{>0})=(p+\lambda)\star. \label{shift:seidel}$$
  - Explanation: Modulo the loop variable and positive base degree, the shift operator is fiber quantum multiplication by the hyperplane class plus the equivariant parameter.
- **claim-shift-spectrum** (classification, [source](#stmt-shift-spectrum-proposition)): For generic $(y,\lambda)$, the distinct eigenvalue branches of $T_a\bmod(z,Q^{>0})$ are $$\lambda+h,\qquad h^r(h+\lambda)=y. \label{shift:spectrum}$$ Each branch may have multiplicity and a nontrivial nilpotent part. For $\lambda\ne0$, near $y=0$ there is one branch tending to zero and a cluster of $r$ branches tending to $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: For generic parameters the eigenvalue branches of the reduced shift are the equivariant parameter plus the roots of the fiber relation. Near zero fiber variable one branch tends to zero and a cluster tends to the parameter. No semisimplicity of the base is used.
- **claim-holomorphic-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-calculus)): For $T$ as above and a scalar holomorphic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(T^{(0)})$, held fixed independently of the parameters, define $$f(T)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm i}\int_\Gamma f(\xi)R_T(\xi)\,d\xi, \label{spec:functional-calculus}$$ where $\Gamma$ encloses the whole spectrum within that neighborhood. The neighborhood may be disconnected. This construction is unital, multiplicative, and compatible with holomorphic composition. In particular, the functions equal to $1$ on one spectral group and $0$ on the others give mutually orthogonal idempotents whose sum is the identity. All these operators commute with $T$.* *If, in addition, $[T,\lambda]=zT$, then $$=z\bigl(\xi f'(\xi)\bigr)(T). \label{spec:functional-commutator}$$ If a differential operator $G$ commutes with $T$, acts continuously on the coefficientwise Laurent extension of <a href="#spec:algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:algebra">[spec:algebra]</a>, and commutes with the scalar spectral variable $\xi$, then $[G,f(T)]=0$.
  - Explanation: A contour-integral functional calculus on completed algebras of differential series is unital, multiplicative and compatible with composition. It yields orthogonal spectral idempotents and commutator identities.
- **claim-resolvent-gauge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-gauge)): Let $T$ and $T'$ have coefficientwise resolvents on a common contour, constructed as in Lemma <a href="#spec:calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:calculus">16</a>. Suppose these resolvents belong to the same algebra <a href="#spec:laurent-algebra" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:laurent-algebra">[spec:laurent-algebra]</a>. If $M$ and $M^{-1}$ belong to that algebra, are independent of $\xi$, and $T'=MTM^{-1}$, then $$R_{T'}(\xi)=M R_T(\xi)M^{-1},\qquad f(T')=M f(T)M^{-1}$$ for every function represented by that contour calculus.
  - Explanation: Resolvents and functions of an operator transform by conjugation under a change of frame that lies in a common Laurent algebra.
- **claim-block-commutators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-commutators)): The operators in <a href="#spec:projectors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:projectors">[spec:projectors]</a> satisfy $$=0,\qquad [\mathscr L_i,\lambda]=zP_i,\qquad [G_a,P_i]=[G_a,\mathscr L_i]=0. \label{spec:block-commutators}$$ In particular $P_i$ and $M_i=\mathscr L_i-zP_i\partial_\lambda$ are matrix series, without coefficient differentiation. The operator $$D_i=P_iG_a-\frac{\lambda}{z}\mathscr L_i \label{spec:block-grading}$$ differentiates only in $z$ and satisfies $P_iD_i=D_iP_i=D_i$.
  - Explanation: The spectral projectors and block logarithms commute with the grading, and the block grading differs from the shifted grading so that coefficient differentiation disappears. The block operator differentiates only in the loop variable.
- **claim-block-modes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-modes)): Define $$A_{-1,i}=z^{-1}P_i,\qquad A_{k,i}=z^{-1}(zD_i)^{k+1}\quad(k\geq0). \label{spec:mode-definition}$$ Each $A_{k,i}$ is a series in powers $z^m$ with $m\geq-1$, whose coefficients are matrix differential operators in $z\partial_z$ of order at most $k+1$. Its coefficients contain no derivatives in $y,\lambda$ or $Q$. Each fixed base and loop coefficient continues holomorphically along paths in the generic locus of <a href="#spec:eigenvalues" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:eigenvalues">[spec:eigenvalues]</a>, with the spectral labels and logarithms continued along the path.* *For any scalar $b$ independent of $z$, replacing $D_i$ by $D_i+bz^{-1}P_i$ gives $$A_{k,i}\longmapsto \sum_{j=0}^{k+1}\binom{k+1}{j}b^{k+1-j}A_{j-1,i}. \label{spec:binomial}$$ For distinct branches $i\neq j$, one has $(zD_i)(zD_j)=0$. Thus, if $P=\sum_iP_i$ and $D=\sum_iD_i$ for a group of branches, then the modes formed from $P,D$ are the sums of their individual modes.
  - Explanation: Block modes built from the block grading have bounded loop powers and bounded differential order, no derivatives in the parameters, continue along generic paths, and change by a binomial rule when the grading is shifted by a scalar. Modes of distinct branches add.
- **claim-zero-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-zero)): The projectors $P_B,P_X$ and the cluster logarithm $\mathscr L_X$ have holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$. Their Taylor expansions at $y=0$ satisfy $$P_F=S_W\Pi_FS_W^{-1}\quad(F=B,X),\qquad \mathscr L_X=S_W\mathscr L_{d,X}S_W^{-1}, \label{spec:zero-conjugation}$$ where $\mathscr L_{d,X}$ is the logarithm of $K_XE_z$ on the $X$ summand, extended by zero on $B$.* *Moreover, $T_aP_B$ is divisible by $y$ coefficientwise in $z$ and base degree. Set $$\widetilde T_B=y^{-1}T_aP_B. \label{spec:divided-shift}$$ It has holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$. Its nonzero spectral block at $y=z=Q^{>0}=0$ has eigenvalue $(-\lambda)^{-r}$, with possible nilpotent part, and its complementary block is zero. If $\mathscr K_B$ is its supported logarithm on the nonzero block, then, on a punctured sector in $y$, one can choose the $B$-branch logarithm as $$\mathscr L_B=(\log y)P_B+\mathscr K_B. \label{spec:divided-log}$$ The coefficients of $\mathscr K_B$ are holomorphic through $y=0$. Its Taylor expansion, and hence <a href="#spec:divided-log" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:divided-log">[spec:divided-log]</a>, obeys the same conjugation by $S_W$ as in <a href="#spec:zero-conjugation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:zero-conjugation">[spec:zero-conjugation]</a>, with the corresponding fixed-block operators in the descendant frame.
  - Explanation: At zero fiber degree the projectors and the cluster logarithm are holomorphic and are conjugates, by the fundamental solution, of the fixed-component operators. The branch tending to zero has a logarithm with an explicit logarithmic term in the fiber variable.
- **claim-fixed-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-equivalence)): Fix a germ at $\lambda\ne0$. Near $Q=y=0$, use the full Novikov completion with a formal $\log y$ adjoined, allowing polynomial dependence on $\log y$ in each coefficient. Let $F=B$ denote the branch near the eigenvalue zero, or let $F=X$ denote the whole cluster near the eigenvalue $\lambda$. Use the projectors and logarithms near $y=0$ of Proposition <a href="#spec:zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:zero">20</a>, and let $A_{k,F}$ be their modes. Then, as identities over this coefficient ring, $$\mathcal A_W\text{ satisfies every }A_{k,F}\text{ projectively} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad Z_F\text{ satisfies every }\ell_{k,F}\text{ projectively}, \qquad k\geq-1.$$ The operators on the left are infinitesimal symplectic for the equivariant pairing of $W$. On the right, $Z_F$ is the ordinary descendant potential with its full cohomology and actual integral curve labels.
  - Explanation: Projective satisfaction of the block modes by the ancestor potential of the auxiliary space is equivalent to projective satisfaction of the ordinary modes by the descendant potential of the corresponding fixed component.
- **claim-fixed-classical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-classical)): The following identities hold in the formal Laurent calculus of Lemma <a href="#spec:gauge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:gauge">17</a>: $$\begin{aligned} \Delta_F^{-1}G_F\Delta_F &=\ell_{0,F}+\lambda\partial_\lambda+\frac{nw\lambda}{z}, \label{fixed:grading-conjugation}\\ \Delta_F^{-1}T_F\Delta_F &=y^j\exp\left( \frac{\Theta_F(\lambda)-\Theta_F(\lambda+z)}{z} \right)e^{z\partial_\lambda}. \label{fixed:shift-conjugation} \end{aligned}$$ Under the same change of space, the logarithm prescribed near $y=0$ becomes $$\label{fixed:log-conjugation} z\partial_\lambda+j\log y-\Theta_F'(\lambda) =z\partial_\lambda+j\log y+nw\log(w\lambda),$$ up to a scalar constant determined by the logarithm branch.
  - Explanation: Conjugation by the multiplier turns the restricted grading into the ordinary zero mode plus parameter differentiation and a scalar pole, turns the shift into a pure translation flow, and identifies the functional logarithm.
- **claim-qrr-normalized** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized)): With the quantization and dilaton translations of Section <a href="#conv:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:section">2</a>, $$\label{fixed:qrr} Z_{F,\mathrm{ntw}}=(\text{invertible scalar})\, U_{\Delta_{F,0}}Z_F.$$ This is an invertible identity formal in $\lambda^{-1}$ and coefficientwise in the full Novikov variables and $\hbar$.
  - Explanation: Quantum Riemann–Roch relates the descendant potential of the normalized twist to the ordinary potential by a quantized loop transformation, up to an invertible scalar, formally in the inverse equivariant parameter.
- **claim-rank-scaling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fixed-rank-scaling)): Put $m_F=a^{-n}$. The actual inverse Euler twist is obtained from the normalized twist by the substitutions $$\label{fixed:scaling} \hbar\longmapsto\hbar/m_F, \qquad Q^\beta y^l\longmapsto Q^\beta y^l a^{-\int_d c_N},$$ where $d$ is the corresponding actual class on $F$. Its pairing is $\eta_F^t=m_F\eta_F^{\mathrm{ntw}}$. Under these substitutions the quadratic quantizations of $\Delta_{F,0}\ell_{k,F}\Delta_{F,0}^{-1}$, for every $k\geq-1$, in the two paired spaces agree.
  - Explanation: The actual inverse Euler twist differs from the normalized twist by a rescaling of the genus parameter and of curve variables, and a rescaled pairing, under which the quantizations of the conjugated modes agree.
- **claim-ancestor-polynomiality** (bound, [source](#stmt-cont-polynomiality)): Fix a genus $g$, an actual base class $\beta$, and a list of insertions $b_i\bar\psi_i^{a_i}$, $1\leq i\leq n$. Their connected invariant in $W$, summed over fiber degree with weight $y^l$, is polynomial in $y$ and $\lambda$. It vanishes unless the distinguished curve is stable and $$\sum_i a_i\leq 3g-3+n. \label{cont:ancestor-bound}$$ The latter bound is independent of $\beta,l,\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Connected ancestor invariants of the auxiliary space are polynomial in the fiber variable and equivariant parameter at fixed base class, and vanish when total ancestor power exceeds the dimension bound, independently of fiber degree.
- **claim-finite-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cont-finite-tests)): Fix $k\geq-1$, a power $\hbar^{G-1}$, a base class $\beta$, and an insertion monomial of degree $N$ in <a href="#cont:equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cont:equation">[cont:equation]</a>. Its coefficient is a finite sum of holomorphic germs on the spectral covering of the generic $(y,\lambda)$ locus, with the chosen logarithms. It continues along every path in that locus. Near $y=0$, the coefficient for $\mathcal E_{k,B}$ is a finite polynomial in $\log y$ with coefficients holomorphic through zero; the coefficient for the cluster expression $\mathcal E_{k,X}$ is holomorphic through zero. In these two cases its Taylor expansion is the corresponding formal Novikov coefficient test.
  - Explanation: Each coefficient of a mode equation is a finite sum of holomorphic germs on the spectral covering, continues along every generic path, and near zero is logarithmic-polynomial for the base branch and holomorphic for the cluster. Formal identities therefore become identities of germs.
- **claim-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cont-monodromy)): The monodromy of <a href="#cont:cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cont:cover">[cont:cover]</a> acts transitively on its $r+1$ branches.
  - Explanation: The spectral covering over the complement of its critical values is connected, so its monodromy permutes the branches transitively.
- **claim-projective-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cont-projective-transfer)): If the ordinary descendant potential of $B$ satisfies its ordinary modes projectively, then the same is true for $X$.
  - Explanation: Projective satisfaction of the ordinary modes on the base continues by monodromy to every branch, is summed over the cluster, and returns through zero fiber degree to give projective satisfaction on the projective bundle.
- **claim-virasoro-commutators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cont-commutators)): For every smooth projective complex variety $Y$, with the operators of Section <a href="#conv:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:section">2</a>, one has $$=-2L_0^Y,\qquad [L_0^Y,L_k^Y]=-kL_k^Y\quad(k\geq-1,\ k\neq0). \label{cont:exact-commutators}$$
  - Explanation: The quantized ordinary operators satisfy the Virasoro commutation relations exactly, with the scalar from contractions producing the prescribed constant in the zero mode.
- **claim-exact-constant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cont-exact)): Projective satisfaction of all the ordinary modes by $Z_Y$ implies $\mathsf V(Y)$ with exactly the constant specified in $L_0^Y$.
  - Explanation: Insertion-independent scalars are removed by the commutation relations, so projective satisfaction of all ordinary modes gives the exact constraints with the specified zero-mode constant.

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-leg-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "A moving leg has degree $m\\geq1$ on a line joining a point $x\\in X$ to its image $b=\\pi(x)\\in B$. Its map from $\\mathbb P^1$ is totally ramified over $x$ and $b$, and its markings and nodes lie at these two points. For any specified marking and attachment status at the endpoints, the leg moduli is a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack, the $\\mu_m$-gerbe $q_m:\\mathfrak L_m\\to X$ of $m$th roots of $\\mathcal O_X(-1)$. Its endpoint evaluation maps are $q_m$ and $\\pi\\circ q_m:\\mathfrak L_m\\to B$.*\n\n*At an endpoint in $F$, the tangent line of the leg has equivariant first Chern class $$a=\\frac{w_F\\lambda}{m}+\\nu,$$ where $\\nu$ is an ordinary degree-two class on the leg stack.         "source_id": "PM-065.1"

        "exact_quote": "There is a shift operator $$T_a=A(z,\\lambda,Q,y)e^{z\\partial_\\lambda}$$ on the small quantum-cohomology module of $W$, whose matrix $A$ is polynomial in $z,\\lambda$ at every curve coefficient in the chosen equivariant basis.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "For $T$ as above and a scalar holomorphic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}(T^{(0)})$, held fixed independently of the parameters, define $$f(T)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi\\mathrm i}\\int_\\Gamma\n                  f(\\xi)R_T(\\xi)\\,d\\xi,\n \\label{spec:functional-calculus}$$ where $\\Gamma$ encloses the whole spectrum within that neighborhood. The neighborhood may be disconnected. This construction is unital, multiplicative, and compatible with holomorphic composition. In particular, the functions equal to $1$ on one spectral group and $0$ on the others give mutually orthogonal idempotents whose sum is the identity. All these operators commute with $T$.*\n\n\n*If, in addition, $[T,\\lambda]=zT$, then $$=z\\bigl(\\xi f'(\\xi)\\bigr)(T).\n \\label{spec:functional-commutator}$$ If a differential operator $G$ commutes with $T$, acts continuously on the coefficientwise Laurent extension of <a href=\"#spec:algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:algebra\">[spec:algebra]</a>, and commutes with the scalar spectral variable $\\xi$, then $[G,f(T)]=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "For $T$ as above and a scalar holomorphic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}(T^{(0)})$, held fixed independently of the parameters, define $$f(T)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi\\mathrm i}\\int_\\Gamma\n                  f(\\xi)R_T(\\xi)\\,d\\xi,\n \\label{spec:functional-calculus}$$ where $\\Gamma$ encloses the whole spectrum within that neighborhood. The neighborhood may be disconnected. This construction is unital, multiplicative, and compatible with holomorphic composition. In particular, the functions equal to $1$ on one spectral group and $0$ on the others give mutually orthogonal idempotents whose sum is the identity. All these operators commute with $T$.*\n\n\n*If, in addition, $[T,\\lambda]=zT$, then $$=z\\bigl(\\xi f'(\\xi)\\bigr)(T).\n \\label{spec:functional-commutator}$$ If a differential operator $G$ commutes with $T$, acts continuously on the coefficientwise Laurent extension of <a href=\"#spec:algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:algebra\">[spec:algebra]</a>, and commutes with the scalar spectral variable $\\xi$, then $[G,f(T)]=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For $T$ as above and a scalar holomorphic function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}(T^{(0)})$, held fixed independently of the parameters, define $$f(T)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi\\mathrm i}\\int_\\Gamma\n                  f(\\xi)R_T(\\xi)\\,d\\xi,\n \\label{spec:functional-calculus}$$ where $\\Gamma$ encloses the whole spectrum within that neighborhood.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-gauge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ and $T'$ have coefficientwise resolvents on a common contour, constructed as in Lemma <a href=\"#spec:calculus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:calculus\">16</a>. Suppose these resolvents belong to the same algebra <a href=\"#spec:laurent-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:laurent-algebra\">[spec:laurent-algebra]</a>. If $M$ and $M^{-1}$ belong to that algebra, are independent of $\\xi$, and $T'=MTM^{-1}$, then $$R_{T'}(\\xi)=M R_T(\\xi)M^{-1},\\qquad\n f(T')=M f(T)M^{-1}$$ for every function represented by that contour calculus.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-gauge",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ and $T'$ have coefficientwise resolvents on a common contour, constructed as in Lemma <a href=\"#spec:calculus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:calculus\">16</a>. Suppose these resolvents belong to the same algebra <a href=\"#spec:laurent-algebra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:laurent-algebra\">[spec:laurent-algebra]</a>. If $M$ and $M^{-1}$ belong to that algebra, are independent of $\\xi$, and $T'=MTM^{-1}$, then $$R_{T'}(\\xi)=M R_T(\\xi)M^{-1},\\qquad\n f(T')=M f(T)M^{-1}$$ for every function represented by that contour calculus.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-commutators",
        "exact_quote": "The operators in <a href=\"#spec:projectors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:projectors\">[spec:projectors]</a> satisfy $$=0,\\qquad\n [\\mathscr L_i,\\lambda]=zP_i,\\qquad\n [G_a,P_i]=[G_a,\\mathscr L_i]=0.\n \\label{spec:block-commutators}$$ In particular $P_i$ and $M_i=\\mathscr L_i-zP_i\\partial_\\lambda$ are matrix series, without coefficient differentiation. The operator $$D_i=P_iG_a-\\frac{\\lambda}{z}\\mathscr L_i\n \\label{spec:block-grading}$$ differentiates only in $z$ and satisfies $P_iD_i=D_iP_i=D_i$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-modes",
        "exact_quote": "Define $$A_{-1,i}=z^{-1}P_i,\\qquad\n A_{k,i}=z^{-1}(zD_i)^{k+1}\\quad(k\\geq0).\n \\label{spec:mode-definition}$$ Each $A_{k,i}$ is a series in powers $z^m$ with $m\\geq-1$, whose coefficients are matrix differential operators in $z\\partial_z$ of order at most $k+1$. Its coefficients contain no derivatives in $y,\\lambda$ or $Q$. Each fixed base and loop coefficient continues holomorphically along paths in the generic locus of <a href=\"#spec:eigenvalues\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:eigenvalues\">[spec:eigenvalues]</a>, with the spectral labels and logarithms continued along the path.*\n\n\n*For any scalar $b$ independent of $z$, replacing $D_i$ by $D_i+bz^{-1}P_i$ gives $$A_{k,i}\\longmapsto\n   \\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}\\binom{k+1}{j}b^{k+1-j}A_{j-1,i}.\n \\label{spec:binomial}$$ For distinct branches $i\\neq j$, one has $(zD_i)(zD_j)=0$. Thus, if $P=\\sum_iP_i$ and $D=\\sum_iD_i$ for a group of branches, then the modes formed from $P,D$ are the sums of their individual modes.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-zero",
        "exact_quote": "The projectors $P_B,P_X$ and the cluster logarithm $\\mathscr L_X$ have holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$. Their Taylor expansions at $y=0$ satisfy $$P_F=S_W\\Pi_FS_W^{-1}\\quad(F=B,X),\\qquad\n \\mathscr L_X=S_W\\mathscr L_{d,X}S_W^{-1},\n \\label{spec:zero-conjugation}$$ where $\\mathscr L_{d,X}$ is the logarithm of $K_XE_z$ on the $X$ summand, extended by zero on $B$.*\n\n\n*Moreover, $T_aP_B$ is divisible by $y$ coefficientwise in $z$ and base degree. Set $$\\widetilde T_B=y^{-1}T_aP_B.\n \\label{spec:divided-shift}$$ It has holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$. Its nonzero spectral block at $y=z=Q^{>0}=0$ has eigenvalue $(-\\lambda)^{-r}$, with possible nilpotent part, and its complementary block is zero. If $\\mathscr K_B$ is its supported logarithm on the nonzero block, then, on a punctured sector in $y$, one can choose the $B$-branch logarithm as $$\\mathscr L_B=(\\log y)P_B+\\mathscr K_B.\n \\label{spec:divided-log}$$ The coefficients of $\\mathscr K_B$ are holomorphic through $y=0$. Its Taylor expansion, and hence <a href=\"#spec:divided-log\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:divided-log\">[spec:divided-log]</a>, obeys the same conjugation by $S_W$ as in <a href=\"#spec:zero-conjugation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:zero-conjugation\">[spec:zero-conjugation]</a>, with the corresponding fixed-block operators in the descendant frame.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-zero",

Set $$\\widetilde T_B=y^{-1}T_aP_B.\n \\label{spec:divided-shift}$$ It has holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$.         "anchor": "stmt-fixed-equivalence",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a germ at $\\lambda\\ne0$. Near $Q=y=0$, use the full Novikov completion with a formal $\\log y$ adjoined, allowing polynomial dependence on $\\log y$ in each coefficient. Let $F=B$ denote the branch near the eigenvalue zero, or let $F=X$ denote the whole cluster near the eigenvalue $\\lambda$. Use the projectors and logarithms near $y=0$ of Proposition <a href=\"#spec:zero\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:zero\">20</a>, and let $A_{k,F}$ be their modes. Then, as identities over this coefficient ring, $$\\mathcal A_W\\text{ satisfies every }A_{k,F}\\text{ projectively}\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n Z_F\\text{ satisfies every }\\ell_{k,F}\\text{ projectively},\n \\qquad k\\geq-1.$$ The operators on the left are infinitesimal symplectic for the equivariant pairing of $W$. On the right, $Z_F$ is the ordinary descendant potential with its full cohomology and actual integral curve labels.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fixed-classical",
        "exact_quote": "The following identities hold in the formal Laurent calculus of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:gauge\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:gauge\">17</a>: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_F^{-1}G_F\\Delta_F\n  &=\\ell_{0,F}+\\lambda\\partial_\\lambda+\\frac{nw\\lambda}{z},\n       \\label{fixed:grading-conjugation}\\\\\n \\Delta_F^{-1}T_F\\Delta_F\n  &=y^j\\exp\\left(\n       \\frac{\\Theta_F(\\lambda)-\\Theta_F(\\lambda+z)}{z}\n                  \\right)e^{z\\partial_\\lambda}.\n       \\label{fixed:shift-conjugation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Under the same change of space, the logarithm prescribed near $y=0$ becomes $$\\label{fixed:log-conjugation}\n z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y-\\Theta_F'(\\lambda)\n   =z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y+nw\\log(w\\lambda),$$ up to a scalar constant determined by the logarithm branch.",
        "source_id": "PM-065.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fixed-classical",
          "exact_quote": "The following identities hold in the formal Laurent calculus of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:gauge\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:gauge\">17</a>: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_F^{-1}G_F\\Delta_F\n  &=\\ell_{0,F}+\\lambda\\partial_\\lambda+\\frac{nw\\lambda}{z},\n       \\label{fixed:grading-conjugation}\\\\\n \\Delta_F^{-1}T_F\\Delta_F\n  &=y^j\\exp\\left(\n       \\frac{\\Theta_F(\\lambda)-\\Theta_F(\\lambda+z)}{z}\n                  \\right)e^{z\\partial_\\lambda}.\n       \\label{fixed:shift-conjugation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Under the same change of space, the logarithm prescribed near $y=0$ becomes $$\\label{fixed:log-conjugation}\n z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y-\\Theta_F'(\\lambda)\n   =z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y+nw\\log(w\\lambda),$$ up to a scalar constant determined by the logarithm branch.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The following identities hold in the formal Laurent calculus of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:gauge\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:gauge\">17</a>: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_F^{-1}G_F\\Delta_F\n  &=\\ell_{0,F}+\\lambda\\partial_\\lambda+\\frac{nw\\lambda}{z},\n       \\label{fixed:grading-conjugation}\\\\\n \\Delta_F^{-1}T_F\\Delta_F\n  &=y^j\\exp\\left(\n       \\frac{\\Theta_F(\\lambda)-\\Theta_F(\\lambda+z)}{z}\n                  \\right)e^{z\\partial_\\lambda}.\n       \\label{fixed:shift-conjugation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Under the same change of space, the logarithm prescribed near $y=0$ becomes $$\\label{fixed:log-conjugation}\n z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y-\\Theta_F'(\\lambda)\n   =z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y+nw\\log(w\\lambda),$$ up to a scalar constant determined by the logarithm branch.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized",
        "exact_quote": "With the quantization and dilaton translations of Section <a href=\"#conv:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conv:section\">2</a>, $$\\label{fixed:qrr}\n Z_{F,\\mathrm{ntw}}=(\\text{invertible scalar})\\,\n                     U_{\\Delta_{F,0}}Z_F.$$ This is an invertible identity formal in $\\lambda^{-1}$ and coefficientwise in the full Novikov variables and $\\hbar$.",

          "source_id": "PM-065.1"

      "exact_statement": "With the quantization and dilaton translations of Section <a href=\"#conv:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conv:section\">2</a>, $$\\label{fixed:qrr}\n Z_{F,\\mathrm{ntw}}=(\\text{invertible scalar})\\,\n                     U_{\\Delta_{F,0}}Z_F.$$ This is an invertible identity formal in $\\lambda^{-1}$ and coefficientwise in the full Novikov variables and $\\hbar$.",
      "explanation": "Quantum Riemann–Roch relates the descendant potential of the normalized twist to the ordinary potential by a quantized loop transformation, up to an invertible scalar, formally in the inverse equivariant parameter.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **satisfied projectively** ([source](#stmt-conv-projective)): An even infinitesimal symplectic operator $A$ is *satisfied projectively* by a potential $Z$ if $$Z^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)Z$$ is independent of the descendant or ancestor variables.
- **fundamental solution** ([source](#stmt-geo-fundamental)): The fundamental solution $S(u,z)=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}+O(z^{-1})$ is defined by $$\eta(b,S(u,z)a) =\eta(b,a)+\left\langle b,\frac{a}{z-\psi}\right\rangle_{0,u}, \qquad \frac1{z-\psi}=\sum_{j\geq0}\psi^jz^{-j-1}. \label{geo:S-definition}$$ The pairing term supplies the unstable two-point contribution.
- **Virasoro constraints V(Y)** ([source](#intro:section)): Write $\mathsf V(Y)$ for the coefficientwise identities $$L_k^Y Z_Y=0\qquad(k\geq-1).$$
- **zero-mode constant** ([source](#intro:section)): Their zero-mode constant is $C_Y=\chi(Y)/16-\mathop{\mathrm{str}}(\mu_Y^2)/4$, and the other modes have no scalar correction.
- **master space** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Set $$W=\mathbb P_B(E\oplus\mathcal O).$$
- **moving leg** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Call an irreducible component of a torus-fixed stable map a *moving leg* if its image is not contained in $W^T=B\sqcup X$.
- **ancestor class** ([source](#geo:ancestors)): Define $\bar\psi_i=\operatorname{st}_{g,m}^*\psi_i$ for $1\leq i\leq m$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-bundle-rank | rank r of the vector bundle E | ≥ | $2$ | all such bundles |
| q-branch-count | number of spectral branches at generic fiber parameter | = | $r+1$ | generic value of y |
| q-spectral-equation | fiber relation h^r(h+\lambda) | = | $y$ | generic fiber parameter and equivariant parameter |
| q-zero-mode-constant | zero-mode constant C_Y | = | $\chi(Y)/16-\mathop{\mathrm{str}}(\mu_Y^2)/4$ | zero Virasoro mode only |
| q-ancestor-degree-bound | total ancestor power sum of a_i | ≤ | $3g-3+n$ | independent of base class, fiber degree and equivariant parameter |
| q-critical-values | nonzero critical value of the spectral covering | = | $(-r)^r\lambda^{r+1}/(r+1)^{r+1}$ | nonzero equivariant parameter |
| q-line-invariant | degree-one line invariant in a fiber | = | $1$ | independent of the equivariant parameter |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-covariance** [claim](#stmt-conv-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-covariance): Commutators of normally ordered quadratic operators, exponentiated. -> Projective satisfaction is preserved by quantized symplectic changes of frame.
- **step-finite-support** [claim](#stmt-conv-finite-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conv-finite-support): Count the finitely many index pairs in each part of the quadratic operator. -> Mode operators act coefficientwise.
- **step-curve-labels** [claim](#stmt-geo-curve-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-curve-labels): Serre spectral sequence and pairing with the tautological class. -> Integral curve classes split into base class and fiber degree.
- **step-leg-geometry** [claim](#stmt-loc-leg-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-leg-geometry): Classify fixed rational components as covers of orbit lines and identify the moduli as a root gerbe. -> Leg moduli with nonzero smoothing characters.
- **step-vertex-conversion** [claim](#stmt-loc-vertex-conversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-vertex-conversion): Splitting divisor for the difference of cotangent classes and a dimension vanishing argument. -> Descendant flags become dressed ancestor flags.
- **step-ancestor-localization** [claim](#stmt-loc-factorization) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Virtual localization, genus-zero two-point identities, and assembly of stable graphs into an upper-triangular quantization. -> Factorization of the fundamental solution and ancestor potential through the fixed components.
- **step-grading-calibration** [claim](#stmt-shift-grading-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-grading-lemma): Homogeneity of two-point invariants and the divisor equation. -> Ancestor grading is conjugate to the descendant grading.
- **step-shift-operator** [claim](#stmt-shift-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-operator): Section invariants on the associated bundle over the projective line, using Iritani's shift theorem. -> Shift operator with explicit fixed-locus form.
- **step-shift-polynomiality** [claim](#stmt-shift-polynomiality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-polynomiality): Virtual dimension grows with fiber degree, so only finitely many fiber degrees contribute. -> Polynomial coefficients in the fiber variable.
- **step-shift-commutation** [claim](#stmt-shift-commutation-lemma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-commutation-lemma): Check in the descendant frame that the contributions of the normal roots cancel. -> Grading and shift commute.
- **step-fiber-shift** [claim](#stmt-shift-fiber-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-fiber-operator): Linearity over base cohomology, commutation with multiplication by the hyperplane class, and a degree count. -> Reduced shift equals multiplication by the shifted hyperplane class.
- **step-shift-spectrum** [claim](#stmt-shift-spectrum-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shift-spectrum-proposition): Filter by powers of the nilpotent base ideal and read the roots from the fiber relation. -> Eigenvalue branches and the cluster structure near zero.
- **step-holomorphic-calculus** [claim](#stmt-spec-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-calculus): Formal resolvent, resolvent identity and scalar Cauchy formula on nested contours. -> Functional calculus with spectral idempotents.
- **step-resolvent-gauge** [claim](#stmt-spec-gauge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-gauge): Conjugation gives a two-sided inverse of the conjugated operator. -> Covariance of resolvents under change of frame.
- **step-block-commutators** [claim](#stmt-spec-commutators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-commutators): Apply the commutator rule of the calculus to the branch indicator and its logarithm. -> Block operators without parameter differentiation.
- **step-block-modes** [claim](#stmt-spec-modes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-modes): Bound loop powers and Euler order, continue coefficients along generic paths, and use the binomial formula. -> Block modes with coefficient bounds.
- **step-zero-comparison** [claim](#stmt-spec-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-zero): Neumann construction at zero fiber degree and conjugation by the fundamental solution. -> Block operators near zero are conjugates of fixed-component operators.
- **step-fixed-classical** [claim](#stmt-fixed-classical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-classical): Bernoulli-number multiplier that cancels the normal-bundle terms in the grading and the shift. -> Conjugated grading, shift and logarithm.
- **step-qrr-normalized** [claim](#stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized): Quantum Riemann–Roch for the normalized characteristic class. -> Normalized twisted potential as a quantized transformation of the ordinary potential.
- **step-rank-scaling** [claim](#stmt-fixed-rank-scaling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-rank-scaling): Riemann–Roch rank count and rescaling of the pairing and genus parameter. -> Passage from normalized to actual inverse Euler twist.
- **step-fixed-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-fixed-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fixed-rank-scaling-2): Chain of invertible comparisons from the ordinary potential to the ancestor potential of the master space. -> Equivalence of projective satisfaction on a fixed component and on the master space branch.
- **step-ancestor-polynomiality** [claim](#stmt-cont-polynomiality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-polynomiality): Dimension bound from the moduli of stable curves and a virtual dimension count in the fiber degree. -> Polynomial ancestor coefficients with a uniform bound.
- **step-finite-tests** [claim](#stmt-cont-finite-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-finite-tests): Each coefficient of a quadratic operator on the potential involves finitely many connected potentials and mode entries. -> Mode equations are finite sums of holomorphic germs.
- **step-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-cont-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-monodromy): Connectedness of the complement of finitely many points in the plane and path lifting. -> Transitive action on the branches.
- **step-projective-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cont-projective-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-projective-transfer): Continue the base-branch equations along loops, sum over the cluster, and return through zero. -> Projective constraints on the projective bundle.
- **step-virasoro-commutators** [claim](#stmt-cont-commutators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-commutators): Classical commutators plus the contraction scalar computed on the super space. -> Exact commutation relations with the prescribed constant.
- **step-exact-constant** [claim](#stmt-cont-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-exact): Commutators annihilate the potential once insertion-independent scalars are present. -> Exact constraints from projective ones.
- **step-main-transfer** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cont-exact-2): Combine projective transfer with exactness of the constant. -> Transfer of the full constraints from the base to the projective bundle.

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-spec-calculus"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-spec-gauge",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-holomorphic-calculus"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-gauge",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ and $T'$ have coefficientwise resolvents on a common contour, constructed as in Lemma <a href=\"#spec:calculus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:calculus\">16</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-resolvent-gauge",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Conjugation gives a two-sided inverse of the conjugated operator.",
      "output": "Covariance of resolvents under change of frame.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-spec-gauge"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-spec-commutators",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-holomorphic-calculus",
        "step-shift-commutation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-commutators",
          "exact_quote": "The operators in <a href=\"#spec:projectors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:projectors\">[spec:projectors]</a> satisfy $$=0,\\qquad\n [\\mathscr L_i,\\lambda]=zP_i,\\qquad\n [G_a,P_i]=[G_a,\\mathscr L_i]=0.\n \\label{spec:block-commutators}$$ In particular $P_i$ and $M_i=\\mathscr L_i-zP_i\\partial_\\lambda$ are matrix series, without coefficient differentiation.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-block-commutators",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Apply the commutator rule of the calculus to the branch indicator and its logarithm.",
      "output": "Block operators without parameter differentiation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-spec-commutators"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-spec-modes",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-block-commutators"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-modes",
          "exact_quote": "Define $$A_{-1,i}=z^{-1}P_i,\\qquad\n A_{k,i}=z^{-1}(zD_i)^{k+1}\\quad(k\\geq0).\n \\label{spec:mode-definition}$$ Each $A_{k,i}$ is a series in powers $z^m$ with $m\\geq-1$, whose coefficients are matrix differential operators in $z\\partial_z$ of order at most $k+1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-block-modes",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Bound loop powers and Euler order, continue coefficients along generic paths, and use the binomial formula.",
      "output": "Block modes with coefficient bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-spec-modes"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-spec-zero",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-resolvent-gauge",
        "step-block-modes",
        "step-ancestor-localization",
        "step-shift-spectrum"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-zero",
          "exact_quote": "The projectors $P_B,P_X$ and the cluster logarithm $\\mathscr L_X$ have holomorphic coefficient germs through $y=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-zero-comparison",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Neumann construction at zero fiber degree and conjugation by the fundamental solution.",
      "output": "Block operators near zero are conjugates of fixed-component operators.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-spec-zero"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-fixed-classical",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-resolvent-gauge"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fixed-classical",
          "exact_quote": "The following identities hold in the formal Laurent calculus of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:gauge\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:gauge\">17</a>: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta_F^{-1}G_F\\Delta_F\n  &=\\ell_{0,F}+\\lambda\\partial_\\lambda+\\frac{nw\\lambda}{z},\n       \\label{fixed:grading-conjugation}\\\\\n \\Delta_F^{-1}T_F\\Delta_F\n  &=y^j\\exp\\left(\n       \\frac{\\Theta_F(\\lambda)-\\Theta_F(\\lambda+z)}{z}\n                  \\right)e^{z\\partial_\\lambda}.\n       \\label{fixed:shift-conjugation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Under the same change of space, the logarithm prescribed near $y=0$ becomes $$\\label{fixed:log-conjugation}\n z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y-\\Theta_F'(\\lambda)\n   =z\\partial_\\lambda+j\\log y+nw\\log(w\\lambda),$$ up to a scalar constant determined by the logarithm branch.",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fixed-classical",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Bernoulli-number multiplier that cancels the normal-bundle terms in the grading and the shift.",
      "output": "Conjugated grading, shift and logarithm.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-fixed-classical"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-covariance"
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized",
          "exact_quote": "With the quantization and dilaton translations of Section <a href=\"#conv:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conv:section\">2</a>, $$\\label{fixed:qrr}\n Z_{F,\\mathrm{ntw}}=(\\text{invertible scalar})\\,\n                     U_{\\Delta_{F,0}}Z_F.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-qrr-normalized",
      "inputs": [],

      "output": "Normalized twisted potential as a quantized transformation of the ordinary potential.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-fixed-qrr-normalized"

      "inputs": [],

          "source_id": "PM-065.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-virasoro-commutators",
      "inputs": [],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-transfer-implication**: Full ordinary descendant Virasoro constraints on a smooth connected projective base imply the same constraints on the projectivization of any algebraic vector bundle of rank at least two over it.
- **inv-projective-satisfaction**: Projective satisfaction of an operator by a potential means the normalized action on the potential is independent of the descendant or ancestor variables, which discards only an insertion-independent scalar.
- **inv-spectral-branches**: For generic parameters the distinct eigenvalue branches of the reduced shift operator are given by the roots of the stated algebraic equation, with one branch near zero and a cluster of the remaining ones near the equivariant parameter.
- **inv-branch-monodromy**: The monodromy of the spectral covering permutes its branches transitively, which allows equations to be transported from the base branch to every branch.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-rank**: Apply the transfer to a line bundle or any bundle of rank below two. -> The theorem and its spectral construction are stated only for rank at least two; nothing is claimed outside that range.
- **anti-unconditional-base**: Conclude the constraints on the total space without establishing them on the base. -> The result is an implication from the base to the total space, not an unconditional statement.
- **anti-nonprojective-base**: Use the transfer for singular, non-projective or non-complex bases. -> The pairing, grading and stable-map foundations used are set up only for the stated class of bases.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an implication between identities of generating functions of descendant invariants, proved by localization, spectral calculus, analytic continuation and monodromy; it states no algorithm or finite explicit construction that a program could compute, as the theorem asserts only that one family of constraints implies another.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an implication between Virasoro constraints on two geometric potentials. It gives no procedure, interface or data flow that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The statement concerns identities among formal generating series on moduli of stable maps. It has no dynamics, state evolution or simulable system. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computable object is delivered. The spectral modes are built coefficientwise inside completed algebras and are not packaged as something a tool could evaluate. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof argues by localization, spectral separation and analytic continuation along monodromy. These steps are existential and structural, with no finite procedure and no stated complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no representation of data to be stored or queried. Its objects are completed operator algebras and cohomology spaces. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no numerical kernel, fixed-size arithmetic or dataflow in the source. Nothing in it maps to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no hardness assumption, primitive, adversary or security notion. Its content is cohomological and operator-theoretic. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, model class or learning guarantee. The identities hold for geometric generating functions, not statistical estimators. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result states no objective, constraint set or optimum. The branch equation in the proof is a covering of a parameter line, not an optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages or failure models in the source. The word transport refers to continuing identities along paths in a parameter space. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main value is theoretical, as a transfer principle for towers of projective bundles in enumerative geometry and mathematical physics. It is not an engineering construction, and its use stays conditional on the base constraints. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-base-constraints** (hypothesis): The theorem is only an implication: the full ordinary descendant constraints must already hold on the base. Nothing is asserted about the total space when the base constraints are unknown. Blocked use: ['Concluding the constraints on a projective bundle without establishing them on its base']
- **limit-rank** (hypothesis): The bundle must have rank at least two. The spectral branch count and the master-space construction are set up under this hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the transfer to a line bundle or to any bundle of rank below two']
- **limit-base-class** (hypothesis): The base must be a smooth connected projective variety over the complex numbers. The pairing, Hodge grading and stable-map moduli used in the quantization are set up for this class only. Blocked use: ['Using the result for singular, non-projective or non-complex bases']
- **limit-ordinary-theory** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns the ordinary theory of each total space, with the ordinary descendant potential and its Virasoro operators. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a statement about other variants of the theory such as open or orbifold invariants']
- **limit-ancestor-finiteness** (approximation): The continuation of equations is coefficientwise: each test involves finitely many coefficients, and no convergence of a whole potential or of the loop and Novikov series is asserted. Blocked use: ['Treating the identities as analytic statements about convergent total potentials']
- **limit-genericity** (edge_case): The eigenvalue description of the shift holds for generic parameters, and the continuation uses only the generic locus away from the critical values of the covering. Blocked use: ['Reading the branch description as valid at the critical values of the covering']
- **limit-projective-scalar** (formal_scope): Projective satisfaction discards an insertion-independent scalar. The exact constant in the zero mode is recovered only through the commutation relations at the end. Blocked use: ['Treating projective satisfaction alone as the exact constraints with the prescribed constant']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that full ordinary descendant Virasoro constraints pass from a smooth projective complex base to the projectivization of any algebraic vector bundle of rank at least two. The bundle need not split and satisfies no positivity requirement. Assuming the full constraints on the base, the conclusion includes every genus, each individual integral curve class, and all cohomology insertions, including primitive and odd classes. The result also applies successively to towers of projective bundles.

<a id="intro:section"></a>
### Introduction

Virasoro constraints organize the descendant Gromov–Witten invariants of a smooth projective variety into differential equations for a single generating function. A basic structural question is whether these equations pass from a base to the total space of a geometric fibration. We prove that they pass through projectivization of an arbitrary algebraic vector bundle.

For a smooth connected projective complex variety $Y$, let $Z_Y$ be its total ordinary descendant potential: the exponential of the connected stable-map potentials, with genus weight $\hbar^{g-1}$, ordinary cotangent-line classes at the markings, and monomials $Q^d$ indexed by the actual effective classes $d\in H_2(Y;\mathbb Z)$. We use all of $H^*(Y;\mathbb C)$ with its cohomological parity and Poincaré pairing. The Virasoro operators are the normally ordered operators associated to the first Hodge grading $$\mu_Y|_{H^{p,q}(Y)}
       =\bigl(p-\tfrac12\dim_\mathbb CY\bigr)\mathop{\mathrm{id}},
 \qquad R_Y=c_1(TY)\cup.$$ Their zero-mode constant is $C_Y=\chi(Y)/16-\mathop{\mathrm{str}}(\mu_Y^2)/4$, and the other modes have no scalar correction. Section <a href="#conv:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conv:section">2</a> specifies the loop-space and quantization conventions completely. Write $\mathsf V(Y)$ for the coefficientwise identities $$L_k^Y Z_Y=0\qquad(k\geq-1).$$ Thus $\mathsf V(Y)$ includes every genus, individual integral curve class, and finite list of descendants, including primitive and odd insertions.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $B$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$, and let $E$ be an algebraic vector bundle of rank $r\geq2$ on $B$. Let $X=\mathbb P_B(E)$ parametrize one-dimensional subspaces of the fibers of $E$. Then $$\mathsf V(B)\ \Longrightarrow\ \mathsf V(X).$$*

</div>

The bundle is arbitrary: it need not split or admit a filtration by line bundles, and it is subject to no positivity condition. The base is allowed to have nonsemisimple quantum cohomology and arbitrary Hodge types. In particular the assertion applies successively to towers of projective bundles once the constraints hold on the initial base. The conclusion concerns the ordinary theory of each total space.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context and related results

The Virasoro conjecture in Gromov–Witten theory extends the differential constraints for intersection numbers on moduli spaces of stable curves arising in Witten’s conjecture and Kontsevich’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Witten](#ref-Witten), [Kontsevich](#ref-Kontsevich)]. Eguchi–Hori–Xiong proposed the corresponding constraints for quantum cohomology <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[EHX](#ref-EHX)]. The extension to general Hodge types and odd cohomology, including Katz’s correction of the grading, is described by Eguchi–Jinzenji–Xiong <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[EJX](#ref-EJX)]; see also Getzler’s account <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GetzlerVirasoro](#ref-GetzlerVirasoro)]. Liu–Tian prove the constraints in genus zero <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LiuTian](#ref-LiuTian)]. In all genera, Givental’s quantization formalism and Teleman’s reconstruction theorem establish them for targets with generically semisimple quantum cohomology <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GiventalQuant](#ref-GiventalQuant), [GiventalSemisimple](#ref-GiventalSemisimple), [Teleman](#ref-Teleman)]. Okounkov–Pandharipande prove them for smooth target curves, including odd insertions <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[OkounkovPandharipande](#ref-OkounkovPandharipande)]. Together with Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, this gives the full constraints for every projective bundle over a smooth projective curve, and for towers of such bundles.

The closest transfer theorem is due to Coates–Givental–Tseng <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CGT](#ref-CGT)]. They prove that the Virasoro constraints hold for the base of a toric bundle if and only if they hold for its total space, for toric bundles constructed from sums of line bundles. Their proof combines ancestor localization with Brown’s mirror theorem <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Brown](#ref-Brown)]; split projective bundles are among its examples. For arbitrary vector bundles, Fan <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Fan](#ref-Fan)] proves all-genus reconstruction and Chern-class dependence of projective-bundle invariants. His argument uses the projective completion $\mathbb P_B(E\oplus\mathcal O)$, which is also the auxiliary space used below. Reconstruction of invariants by itself does not establish compatibility with the Virasoro differential operators.

Iritani–Koto <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[IritaniKoto](#ref-IritaniKoto)] construct a mirror theorem and a decomposition of the quantum $D$-module for arbitrary projective bundles. Koto <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Koto](#ref-Koto)] proves a mirror theorem for nonsplit toric bundles. These results concern genus zero. In particular, Iritani–Koto’s equivalence of generic semisimplicity for a projective bundle and its base, combined with Givental–Teleman, already gives the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> when the base has generically semisimple quantum cohomology. The transfer proved here also covers bases whose quantum cohomology is not semisimple.

As in Fan’s reconstruction, we use a projective completion of the bundle as an auxiliary space. The localization calculation adapts the ancestor organization of Coates–Givental–Tseng, including their use of the genus-zero cone and ancestor identities. We give the additional gluing argument required when the orbit lines are parametrized by a positive-dimensional variety. The mechanism for passing constraints between the two fixed components uses Iritani’s equivariant shift operator <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[IritaniShift](#ref-IritaniShift)]. A local logarithm of that shift cancels equivariant differentiation in the grading. The resulting spectral modes can be quantized and their equations continued between the components. Quantum Riemann–Roch <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[QRR](#ref-QRR)] identifies the local equations with the ordinary constraints. The argument uses virtual localization <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GP](#ref-GP)] and the ancestor–descendant formalism <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[KontsevichManin](#ref-KontsevichManin), [Getzler](#ref-Getzler), [GiventalQuant](#ref-GiventalQuant)]; the necessary forms of these results are recalled at their points of use.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof mechanism

Twisting $E$ by a sufficiently negative line bundle leaves $X$ unchanged and makes $E^*$ globally generated. Set $$W=\mathbb P_B(E\oplus\mathcal O).$$ Let $\mathbb C^*$ scale the trivial summand, and let $\lambda$ be its equivariant parameter. Its fixed components are $B$ and $X$. Write $y$ for the variable measuring tautological degree and retain separate variables $Q^\beta$ for the full integral base classes. The proof has five stages.

1.  *Express the auxiliary ancestors using the two fixed theories.* Localization factors the ancestor potential of $W$ through the product of the inverse-Euler-twisted ancestor potentials of $B$ and $X$. The transformation is the quantization of an upper symplectic series $R(z)$. The same $R$ factors the genus-zero fundamental solution. Section <a href="#loc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:section">4</a> proves both statements together, keeping the families of orbit lines as evaluation correspondences. Localization provides a relation involving both fixed theories; a second structure is needed to transfer constraints from one to the other.

2.  *Separate the shift operator into spectral blocks.* The genus-zero shift operator translates $\lambda$ by the loop variable $z$. Its coefficients, at each base degree, are polynomial in $y$. After reducing modulo $z$ and positive base degree, its distinct spectral values are $\lambda+h$, where $$h^r(h+\lambda)=y.$$ There are $r+1$ branches at a generic value of $y$. Near $y=0$, one tends to the eigenvalue zero and corresponds to $B$; the other $r$ tend to $\lambda$ and together correspond to $X$. Section <a href="#shift:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shift:section">5</a> establishes this description without diagonalizing the quantum cohomology of $B$.

3.  *Construct quantizable modes on the blocks.* The equivariant grading contains $\lambda\partial_\lambda$. On each block the logarithm of the shift contains $z\partial_\lambda$. Subtracting $\lambda/z$ times this logarithm removes coefficient differentiation. The remaining grading defines Virasoro-type loop operators. Section <a href="#spec:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:section">6</a> constructs their projections and logarithms coefficientwise and compares them at $y=0$ with the fixed-component operators. Section <a href="#fixed:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fixed:section">7</a> uses quantum Riemann–Roch to identify the latter, up to a triangular change of modes, with the ordinary modes on $B$ or $X$.

4.  *Continue equations between branches.* For fixed genus, base class, and insertions, the connected ancestor invariants of $W$ are polynomial in $y$. Moreover, ancestor powers are bounded independently of fiber degree. Every coefficient of a quantized equation therefore involves finitely many coefficients of the spectral modes and is an actual identity of germs. The covering $h\mapsto h^r(h+\lambda)$ is connected off its branch values. Its monodromy transports the equations from the $B$ branch to every branch. Adding the $r$ branches of the $X$ cluster and returning to $y=0$ gives the ordinary equations for $X$ up to scalar.

5.  *Fix the scalar normalization.* Quantization is projective, so its covariance has only determined the equations up to insertion-independent scalars. The commutators $[L_{-1},L_1]=-2L_0$ and $[L_0,L_k]=-kL_k$ remove those scalars and give exactly the prescribed constant in $L_0$. Section <a href="#cont:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cont:section">8</a> proves this calculation and completes the transfer.

Two features of the argument are useful beyond the localization formula itself. Subtracting the block logarithm of a difference operator can turn a grading that differentiates parameters into a loop operator over the coefficient ring. Ancestor bounds then let identities for such operators continue coefficientwise, even when continuation of an entire quantized transformation has not been defined. In the present setting these two steps supply the passage from the ordinary constraints on one fixed component to those on the other.

<a id="conv:section"></a>
### Descendant potentials and quantization

We fix the ordinary theory and its operator normalization before introducing the auxiliary equivariant target. Quantized changes of frame are naturally defined up to a scalar. We therefore record both the exact Virasoro constraints and the weaker, projective form that can be transported between frames. The scalar ambiguity will be removed at the end of the proof.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Cohomology, curve classes, and descendants

<a id="conv:grading"></a>
Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $d$. Its state space is the full super vector space $H_Y=H^*(Y;\mathbb C)$, with parity given by cohomological degree modulo two and pairing $$\eta_Y(a,b)=(a,b)_Y=\int_Y a\cup b.$$ Every tensor product, permutation, contraction, and derivative below uses the Koszul convention. In particular, the coevaluation tensor is the categorical inverse of this pairing, without an additional parity involution. Define even endomorphisms $$\mu_Y\big|_{H^{a,b}(Y)}=(a-d/2)\mathop{\mathrm{id}},
 \qquad R_Y=c_1(TY)\cup.
 \label{conv:grading}$$ Thus $\mu_Y$ uses the first Hodge index. Poincaré duality and the Hodge type of $c_1(TY)$ give $\mu_Y^*=-\mu_Y$, $R_Y^*=R_Y$, and $[\mu_Y,R_Y]=R_Y$.

Choose a homogeneous Hodge basis $(\phi_a)$ with $\phi_0=1_Y$. For $j\geq0$, let $t_j^a$ have the parity of $\phi_a$, and put $$t_j=\sum_a t_j^a\phi_a,
 \qquad t(z)=\sum_{j\geq0}t_jz^j.$$ These are formal supercommuting variables; the aggregate insertion $t_j$ is even. The connected, ordinary, unreduced descendant potentials are <a id="conv:potentials"></a>$$\begin{split}
 F_g^Y(t)
 &=\sum_{\substack{\beta\ \mathrm{effective}\\m\geq0}}
     \frac{Q^\beta}{m!}
     \int_{[\overline{\mathcal M}_{g,m}(Y,\beta)]^{\mathrm{vir}}}
       \prod_{i=1}^{m}
          \left(\sum_{j\geq0}\psi_i^j\mathop{\mathrm{ev}}_i^*t_j\right),\\
 Z_Y(t)&=\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq0}\hbar^{g-1}F_g^Y(t)\right).
\end{split}
\label{conv:potentials}$$ Only stable maps contribute. Here $\psi_i$ is the cotangent class at the marked point of the stable map. It is not an ancestor class. The exponential includes disconnected domains and the empty domain.

The Novikov symbols retain the actual effective integral classes $\beta\in H_2(Y;\mathbb Z)$, including $\beta=0$; multiplication is $Q^{\beta_1}Q^{\beta_2}=Q^{\beta_1+\beta_2}$. Fixing an ample integral class gives the Novikov completion: only finitely many labels occur below each fixed ample degree. All identities are read coefficientwise in this completion, at finite order in the descendant variables, and with the coefficientwise Laurent interpretation in $\hbar$. In particular, classes are not identified merely because their divisor degrees agree. The finiteness of effective homology labels of bounded degree follows, for example, from the finite-type Chow spaces of cycles of bounded degree in a projective embedding.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The ordinary Virasoro operators

On the loop space and its standard polarization use <a id="conv:polarization"></a>$$\begin{split}
 \mathcal H_Y&=H_Y((z^{-1})),
 \qquad
 \Omega_Y(f,g)=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{z=0}(f(-z),g(z))_Y\,dz,\\
 \mathcal H_Y&=H_Y[z]\oplus z^{-1}H_Y[[z^{-1}]].
\end{split}
\label{conv:polarization}$$ The infinitesimal symplectic operators are <a id="conv:ordinary-modes"></a>$$\ell_{-1,Y}=z^{-1},\qquad
 \ell_{0,Y}=z\partial_z+\frac12+\mu_Y+\frac{R_Y}{z},\qquad
 \ell_{k,Y}=\ell_{0,Y}(z\ell_{0,Y})^k\quad(k\geq1).
 \label{conv:ordinary-modes}$$ Their infinitesimal symplectic property follows from the adjoint identities after <a href="#conv:grading" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="conv:grading">[conv:grading]</a>, including the sign of $z$ in the residue pairing.

For an even infinitesimal symplectic operator $A$ on a paired loop space with pairing $\eta$, write $$\mathcal Q_A(f)=\tfrac12\Omega(f,Af).$$ Use homogeneous Darboux coordinates for the displayed polarization, with positive coordinates $q_j^a$ and dual negative coordinates $p_{j,a}$. Normal ordering quantizes a $pp$ monomial as $\hbar$ times the corresponding second derivative, a $pq$ monomial as the first-order operator with multiplication before differentiation, and a $qq$ monomial as multiplication by that monomial divided by $\hbar$. Derivatives are left derivatives and all reorderings have their graded signs. We denote the resulting operator, after the dilaton translation <a id="conv:dilaton"></a>$$q(z)=t(z)-z1_Y,
                  \label{conv:dilaton}$$ by $\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)$. The negative coordinates $p_{j,a}$ in this paragraph are unrelated to the tautological divisor $p$ introduced below.

With this sign convention the string operator is <a id="conv:string"></a>$$L_{-1}^Y=\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_{\eta_Y}(\ell_{-1,Y})
   =-\frac{\partial}{\partial t_0^0}
     +\sum_{j\geq0,a}t_{j+1}^a\frac{\partial}{\partial t_j^a}
     +\frac{(t_0,t_0)_Y}{2\hbar}.
 \label{conv:string}$$ For all other modes set <a id="conv:normalized-modes"></a>$$\begin{split}
 L_k^Y&=\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_{\eta_Y}(\ell_{k,Y})\quad(k\ne0),\\
 L_0^Y&=\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_{\eta_Y}(\ell_{0,Y})+C_Y,
 \qquad
 C_Y=\frac{\chi(Y)}{16}-\frac14\mathop{\mathrm{str}}(\mu_Y^2).
\end{split}
\label{conv:normalized-modes}$$ The supertrace uses even minus odd cohomological parity. There are no scalar corrections in positive modes. We write $\mathsf V(Y)$ for the full set of identities <a id="conv:virasoro"></a>$$L_k^Y Z_Y=0\qquad(k\geq-1).
                    \label{conv:virasoro}$$ These equations use every genus, every effective integral class, and all insertions in $H_Y$, including odd and primitive classes.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Projective constraints and changes of frame

<a id="stmt-conv-projective"></a>
<div id="conv:projective" class="definition">

**Definition 2**. An even infinitesimal symplectic operator $A$ is *satisfied projectively* by a potential $Z$ if $$Z^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)Z$$ is independent of the descendant or ancestor variables. In this expression $\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)$ acts on $Z$. Such a scalar may depend on the Novikov variables, equivariant parameters, and $\hbar$.

</div>

This formulation discards exactly the scalar ambiguity of quadratic quantization. It is useful for comparing different paired state spaces. For a symplectic map $M$ between them, denote its quantized action, when defined in the completions used here, by $U_M$. Our group-action convention is the one in the following covariance formula. With the Hamiltonian sign above, its infinitesimal form uses $-\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)$ for the action of $\exp(A)$.

<a id="stmt-conv-covariance"></a>
<div id="conv:covariance" class="lemma">

<a id="conv:covariance-equation"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Projective covariance). *Suppose $M$ and $M^{-1}$ admit quantized actions, and $A$ and $MAM^{-1}$ admit coefficientwise quadratic quantizations. If $\eta$ and $\eta'$ are the source and target pairings, then $$U_M\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_\eta(A)U_M^{-1}
        =\mathop{\mathrm{op}}_{\eta'}(MAM^{-1})+\text{a scalar}.
 \label{conv:covariance-equation}$$ Consequently $A$ is satisfied projectively by $Z$ if and only if $MAM^{-1}$ is satisfied projectively by $U_M Z$. Multiplying either potential by an invertible scalar does not change this condition.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-conv-covariance"><summary>Proof of stmt-conv-covariance</summary>

*Proof.* The commutator of two normally ordered quadratic operators is the quantization of the corresponding quadratic Hamiltonian bracket, together with the scalar arising from the double contractions. In super coordinates that scalar is the corresponding supertrace. Exponentiating this identity gives <a href="#conv:covariance-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="conv:covariance-equation">[conv:covariance-equation]</a>; equivalently one can use the projective quantization formalism of <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[GiventalQuant](#ref-GiventalQuant)]. The same argument applies between paired spaces after a linear change of Darboux coordinates. The last assertions follow because these operators do not differentiate coefficient parameters or $\hbar$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Coefficientwise finiteness near zero curve variables

Some later mode matrices have infinitely many positive powers of $z$, and can also contain finite powers of $z\partial_z$. We interpret their residue identities entrywise in the polarized mode coordinates. The arrays need not act on every uncompleted loop-space input; only the coefficientwise actions specified here are used. The following elementary rule specifies the formal setting for their local quantizations; the continuation argument will establish its separate finiteness assertion at generic fiber parameter.

<a id="stmt-conv-finite-support"></a>
<div id="conv:finite-support" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Suppose a potential has finite descendant-index support at fixed curve coefficient, $\hbar$ power, and variable order, and has $\hbar$ powers bounded below at fixed curve coefficient and variable order. Let $A$ be an even infinitesimal symplectic mode operator, linear over the coefficient parameters and independent of $\hbar$.
